For the base case: `z_n == l && l_prev == l_prev`. So if `z_n == l`, then the base case is `emp`.

So the full unfold is:
1. From head: `data_at(head->next, z1) * data_at(head->prev, 0)`
2. Middle nodes: `data_at(z1->next, z2) * data_at(z1->prev, head) * ...`
3. `l_prev` node: `data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, z_{n-1})`
4. Base case: `emp` (since `z_n = l` and `xp = l_prev = yp`)

So the state `dllseg_shape(head, 0, z, l_prev) * data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, z)` is NOT a direct fold of `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` — it's the state where `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` has been unfolded and then the tail part was written as a new `dllseg_shape`.

Specifically:
`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`
= `dllseg_shape(head, 0, z, l_prev) * data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, z)`

Where `z` is the second-to-last node inside the segment. This is valid because the unfold from `head` to `l` can be split at `l_prev`:
- Segment from head to l_prev (exclusive) = `dllseg_shape(head, 0, z, l_prev)`
- Node l_prev itself: `data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, z)`
- This consumes the segment up to l, where l_prev is the last node inside.

So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) = dllseg_shape(head, 0, z, l_prev) * data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, z)` where `z = l_prev->prev`.

Since the loop body accesses `p->prev` (which is `l_prev->prev` when `p = l_prev`), we need `l_prev->prev` to be accessible. If `l_prev` is inside `dllseg_shape(head, 0, ..., l)`, then to access `l_prev->prev`, we need to unfold from `head` all the way to `l_prev`. That's expensive but possible.

Actually, the unfolding IS what QCP's strategies do. They unfold from arg[0] until they reach the desired node. This is handled automatically. So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, l)` should be fine — strategies will unfold from `head` until they reach `p` or `p->prev`.

- arg[1] = 0 (head's prev)
- arg[2] = p->prev (last node inside the segment)
- arg[3] = l (endpoint, NOT in the segment)

This is exactly the issue described in SKILL.md (Cause 1 of dup_data_at_error):
> C field expression in predicate argument: `dllseg_shape(l, prev, p->prev, p)` uses `p->prev` as a C expression. QCP evaluates it...

And the fix is: "Replace the field expression with a fresh existential variable: `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(l, prev, p_prev, p)`."

`data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, y_295) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
= `data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, y_295) * data_at(l->next, y_290) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(y_290, l)`

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, y_295, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(l_prev, y_295)`

= `dllseg_shape(head, 0, y_295, l_prev) * [data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, y_295) * data_at(l->next, y_290) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(y_290, l)]`

The left side is the precondition heap state. The right side is the invariant. Specifically:

Left: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
Right: `EX p_prev, ((&(l->prev)) |-> p_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)`

Note: in the invariant I wrote `p->prev`, but at the entry `p = l`. So the invariant becomes `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l->prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l->prev)`.

The right side has `data_at(&(l->prev), p_prev)` — this is the C field expression `l->prev` being evaluated. And `dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)` where `p_prev` is the existential from `EX p_prev, ...`.

For this to hold:
- `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` (left) must imply `dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)` (right).
- For this, we need `p_prev = l_prev`.
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` (left) must imply `data_at(&(l->prev), p_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, l)`.
- The `data_at(&(l->prev), p_prev)` needs to come from somewhere.

But `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` unfolds to `(l == 0 && emp) || exists t, data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`.

So `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` IS available by unfolding `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. If we unfold once, we get `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`.

But the invariant also needs `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, l)` where `p_prev = l_prev`. The left has `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`. These are the same if `p_prev = l_prev`! (Because `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` and `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, l)` are the same when `p_prev = l_prev`.)

- `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` == `dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)` ✓
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` == `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, l)` ✓

So the field expression `p->prev` causes `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)` to appear in the state, and this needs to be derived from the predicates. But QCP creates this `data_at` from the field expression AND the predicates also contain the same information, potentially causing a dup_data_at or an extra item that can't be eliminated.

Actually wait, the `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)` is created by QCP EVALUATING the field expression. It's part of the right side of the entailment (the invariant). QCP needs to match it against the left side (the program state).

But `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)` has a specific address: `&(p->prev)`. This is the address of the `prev` field of the struct that `p` points to. QCP computes this address. The left side has the predicate state, and QCP needs to find a `data_at` at the same address.

Let me think about this more concretely:

At entail_wit_1:
Left: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
Right: `data_at(&(l->prev), p_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)`

QCP needs to find `data_at(&(l->prev), p_prev)` from the left. The predicate `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` contains `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` when unfolded. The address `&(l->prev)` matches. So `p_prev = l_prev`.

But QCP must unfold `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` to extract `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)`. After unfolding, the remaining state is `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`. This doesn't match `dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)` = `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`.

Unless QCP can keep the original `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` AND also get `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` from it. But this would be a form of "framing" — where the predicate state is not consumed by the extraction.

Actually, I think the issue might be simpler. Maybe the field expression `p->prev` creates a `data_at` on the RIGHT side, and the LEFT side can match it by unfolding `dlistrep_shape`. After matching, both sides have the `data_at`, and then the rest is compared. The `dlistrep_shape` on the left has been consumed by the unfold, but the right also expects `dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)`. So the left would need to have `dlistrep_shape(l, p_prev)` AFTER the unfold, which it doesn't unless folding is available.

OK, let me try a completely different approach. What if I don't use `p->prev` as a field expression at all, and instead keep the split at `l`?

```
    dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) *
```

entail_wit_1 (entry):
Left: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

Before body, state: `data_at(p_284_addr, ..., p_value) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

And `l != 0` still holds.

So the invariant is re-established. ✓

return_wit_1 (exit):
When `p == head`, the loop exits. The function returns `p = head`.
Postcondition: `dlistrep_shape(__return, 0)` = `dlistrep_shape(p, 0)` = `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`.

The invariant at exit: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` with `p = head`.

We need `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev) |-- dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`.

Is this true? Yes! `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` describes a list from head with head->prev = 0, and the split at l. This is equivalent to the full list `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`.

But does QCP know this? It would need to unfold `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)` to match. Unfolding `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`: `data_at(head->next, t) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(t, head)`.

And the left side has `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. Unfolding `dllseg_shape` from head:
1st: `data_at(head->next, z1) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z1, head, l_prev, l)`

So `data_at(head->next, z1)` matches `data_at(head->next, t)`? No — `t` is from the unfold of `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`, and `z1` is from the unfold of `dllseg_shape`. These are different existential names, but they represent the same value (the next node after head). QCP should be able to equate them.

The unfold of `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`:
`data_at(head->next, t) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(t, head)`

And the left side has `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`.

Unfold `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`:
`exists z1, data_at(head->next, z1) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z1, head, l_prev, l)`

So after unfolding both:
Left: `data_at(head->next, z1) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z1, head, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
Right: `data_at(head->next, t) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(t, head)`

Matching `data_at(head->next, z1)` with `data_at(head->next, t)`: `z1 = t`.
Matching `data_at(head->prev, 0)` on both sides.
Remaining left: `dllseg_shape(t, head, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
Remaining right: `dlistrep_shape(t, head)`

These describe the same thing: `dllseg_shape(t, head, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` = `dlistrep_shape(t, head)`.

For this to match, QCP would need to fold `dllseg_shape(t, head, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` into `dlistrep_shape(t, head)`. This is folding the segment + dlist into a single dlistrep_shape, which might or might not be supported.

Actually, let me think about this more carefully. The unfold of dllseg_shape goes step by step from arg[0]:
1st: arg[0] = head → `data_at(head->next, z1) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z1, head, l_prev, l)`
2nd: arg[0] = z1 → `data_at(z1->next, z2) * data_at(z1->prev, head) * dllseg_shape(z2, z1, l_prev, l)`
...
nth: arg[0] = z_{n-1} → `data_at(z_{n-1}->next, l_prev) * data_at(z_{n-1}->prev, ...) * dllseg_shape(l_prev, z_{n-1}, l_prev, l)`
(n+1)th: arg[0] = l_prev → `data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, z_{n-1}) * dllseg_shape(l, l_prev, l_prev, l)`
Base case: `dllseg_shape(l, l_prev, l_prev, l)` = emp (since `l = l` and `l_prev = l_prev`)

So after full unfold: `data_at(l_prev->prev, z_{n-1})` is available. QCP can read `l_prev->prev` as `z_{n-1}`.

But this unfold is many steps. QCP would need to unfold iteratively until it reaches `l_prev`. The strategies support this by applying rule rules for dllseg_shape unfolding repeatedly.

I think the strategies CAN handle this. The dll_nodata strategies include rules for unfolding dllseg_shape step by step.

But what about the verification? After the loop body executes and reads `l_prev->prev`, QCP assigns `p = l_prev->prev = z_{n-1}`. Now the state has:
- All the `data_at` nodes from the full unfold
- The new value of `p`

To re-check the invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`, QCP needs to fold the unfolded `data_at` nodes back into `dllseg_shape` and `dlistrep_shape`. If folding is supported, this works. If not, it fails.

This brings me back to the folding question. Let me just try the invariant and run verification. If it works, great. If not, I'll need to think differently.

Actually, wait. Let me reconsider whether folding is actually needed. In QCP, when checking entailment for invariant preservation:

1. The CURRENT state (after loop body) is the unfolded state (with `data_at` nodes)
2. The invariant is the folded form (`dllseg_shape * dlistrep_shape`)
3. QCP checks if the CURRENT state entails the INVARIANT

If folding is not supported, this fails because the unfolded state has more items than the folded invariant.

But if QCP checks entailment by also unfolding the INVARIANT:
1. Unfold the invariant to match the current state
2. Check that all items in the unfolded invariant match the current state

For this, QCP would need to unfold the invariant UNTIL it matches the current state. If the current state is fully unfolded (all data_at nodes), QCP would need to unfold the invariant completely to match.

But QCP doesn't know how many times to unfold the invariant. It unfolds one step at a time and tries to match.

I think the answer depends on whether QCP's strategies support iterative unfolding of the invariant to match the current state. This is a standard approach in separation logic provers.

There's a "fatal error: Cannot derive the precondition of Memory Read" at line 20:9, which is `p = p -> prev;`. This is the Memory Read error I was worried about.

So the invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` with `p != 0` in PROP doesn't provide enough information to read `p->prev`. Specifically, `p` is not at arg[0] of any shape predicate, so the strategies can't unfold to find `p->prev`.

Actually, let me re-read the Memory Read error from the second attempt. The invariant is `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` with `p != 0 && l != 0`. The loop body reads `p->prev`.

The fix: I need to put whichever node `p` points to at arg[0] of a shape predicate. But `p` changes value! The invariant must have `p` in a shape predicate argument that adapts as `p` changes.

`has_permission(&p)` grants permission to access `*p`, including `p->prev`. But does it cause `dup_data_at_error` with the shape predicates?

```
dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, l_prev)
```

This splits the list at `p`, with `l_prev` being `p->prev`.

At entry (`p = l`): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` — matches precondition! ✓

After one iteration (`p = l_prev`): The invariant is `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(l_prev, l_prev)`.

But `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev)` means: segment from head to l_prev, last node inside = l_prev, endpoint = l_prev.

From the definition: `dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || exists z, ...`

So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev) = (head == l_prev && 0 == l_prev && emp) || ...`

If `head != l_prev`, base case fails. Non-empty case:
`exists z, data_at(head->next, z) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z, head, l_prev, l_prev)`

And recursively: `dllseg_shape(z, head, l_prev, l_prev)` → eventually `dllseg_shape(l_prev, ..., l_prev, l_prev)` → base case `l_prev == l_prev && ... == l_prev` → `emp`.

So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev)` = the segment from head to l_prev with last node l_prev and endpoint l_prev. This means `l_prev->next = l_prev`, which is WRONG (circular!).

So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, p)` with `p = l_prev` doesn't work because the endpoint and last node are the same, implying circularity.

Hmm. The problem is that `dllseg_shape` has the invariant that `yp->next = y`, where yp is the last node inside the segment and y is the endpoint. If yp == y, then `yp->next = yp`, which is circular.

This means `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p)` works ONLY when `p_prev != p`, i.e., when there's at least one node between p_prev and p. But at loop entry, `p_prev = l_prev` and `p = l` — these are different, so it works. After one iteration, `p = l_prev`. The invariant requires `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev)` which is the problem case.

So the invariant MUST evolve as p changes. The segment endpoint MUST NOT be equal to the last node inside. This means `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, p)` fails when `p = l_prev`.

I think the correct approach is to NOT use the segment with `p` as the endpoint. Instead, use the original segment that stays fixed and put `p` somewhere inside it.

When QCP encounters the statement `p = p->prev`, it needs to:

Actually, let me think about WHY the entailment fails. 

The entailment check: STATE |-- INVARIANT

STATE has the precondition predicates. INVARIANT has `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`.

QCP evaluates the invariant: `p->prev` is a field expression, QCP creates `data_at(&(p->prev), ...phi...)` where `phi` is a fresh variable for the value. Both predicates share this variable.

After evaluation, the invariant becomes:
`data_at(&(p->prev), phi) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, phi, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, phi)`

With `p = l`: `data_at(&(l->prev), phi) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, phi, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, phi)`

QCP then tries to match this against the STATE: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`.

If QCP chooses `phi = l_prev`, the matching becomes:
`data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
vs.
`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

The `data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev)` on the right has no match on the left.

Now, QCP could match it by unfolding `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` into `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`. Then `data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev)` matches `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)`.

But unfolding consumes `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`, leaving `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`.

After this match:
Left: `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`
Right: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

Wait, I already matched `data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev)` against `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)`. Both are consumed.

Remaining LEFT: `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`
Remaining RIGHT: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

After matching `dllseg_shape`:
Remaining LEFT: `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
Remaining RIGHT: `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

Unfold the RIGHT:
`dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` → `exists t', data_at(l->next, t') * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t', l)`

Remaining RIGHT: `data_at(l->next, t') * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t', l)`

But `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` was consumed earlier! So this can't match.

Unless QCP uses `data_at(l->next, t)` to match `data_at(l->next, t')` (= `t = t'`), then:
Left: `dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
Right: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)` — still has the consumed data_at.

Yep, the `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` is needed twice — once by the field expression and once by `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`.

This is the fundamental conflict. The field `l->prev` has two consumers in the invariant: the field expression `p->prev` (evaluated to `data_at(&(l->prev), ...)`) and `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` (which contains `data_at(l->prev, ...)` in its definition).

But they describe the SAME heap cell! In separation logic, one cell one owner. The invariant DOUBLE-COUNTS this cell.

The fix from SKILL.md: replace `p->prev` as a field expression with an existential variable:
> Replace the field expression with a fresh existential variable: `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(l, prev, p_prev, p)`. A logical variable like `p_prev` is a pure symbol — it does not trigger any `data_at` creation by QCP.

So the fix is to NOT use `p->prev` as a field expression in the invariant, but instead find a way to express it that doesn't create a `data_at`.

But how? The invariant can't introduce existential variables. Unless... we use the EXISTING logical variable `l_prev`.

The idea: `l_prev` is `p->prev` at loop entry. After the first iteration, `p->prev` has changed. But `l_prev` still refers to the original `l->prev`.

What if the invariant anchors the segment at the original split (`l`) and uses `l_prev` to avoid field expressions?

```
/*@ Inv Assert p != 0 && l != 0 &&
    dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) *
    dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)
 */
```

This is the original second attempt (Memory Read error). The issue is that `p` is not at arg[0] and the Memory Read fails.

But the first attempt (with field expressions) had NO memory read error. Why?

In the first attempt, the field expression `p->prev` in `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p)` evaluated to `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)`. This `data_at` provides the permission to read `p->prev`. That's why there's no Memory Read error.

So the field expression approach works for memory reads but fails for entailment because the `data_at` double-counts a predicate field.

The entailment fails specifically at entry: the `data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev)` needed by the invariant can't be derived from `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` without consuming the `dlistrep_shape`.

What if we can derive it from `dllseg_shape` instead of `dlistrep_shape`?

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` — does this have `l->prev` as a field? No! `dllseg_shape` says `l_prev` is the last node inside the segment. `l` is the endpoint, not inside the segment. `dllseg_shape` does NOT contain `l->prev`.

Wait, but `l->prev = l_prev`. So the segment's last node is `l_prev`, and `l_prev->next = l`. But `l->prev` is `l_prev`, which is the last node inside the segment.

Actually, looking at the definition of dllseg_shape more carefully:
`dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || exists z, data_at(field_addr(x, next), struct list*, z) * data_at(field_addr(x, prev), struct list*, xp) * dllseg_shape(z, x, yp, y)`

The unfold from x gives `data_at(x->next, z) * data_at(x->prev, xp)` and recurses on z.

So when arg[0] becomes `l_prev` (just before the endpoint):
`data_at(l_prev->next, l) * data_at(l_prev->prev, something) * dllseg_shape(l, l_prev, l_prev, l)`

And `dllseg_shape(l, l_prev, l_prev, l)`: base case `(l == l && l_prev == l_prev && emp)` — yep, emp!

So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` does NOT contain `data_at(l->prev, ...)`. It contains `data_at(l_prev->next, l)` and `data_at(l_prev->prev, ...)`. The `l->prev` is NOT covered by dllseg_shape.

So `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` covers `l->prev = l_prev`.

And the field expression `p->prev` when `p = l` evaluates to `data_at(&(l->prev), ...)`, which comes from `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`.

So the conflict IS within `dlistrep_shape` itself — unfolding gives `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)`, but the invariant also needs `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` (intact, not unfolded).

OK I think the only way forward is to find an invariant that:
1. Makes `p->prev` readable
2. Doesn't double-count the `data_at` from the predicate

What if the invariant doesn't include `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` in its original form, but instead unfolds it explicitly?

```
/*@ Inv Assert p != 0 && l != 0 &&
    dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) *
    data_at(l->prev, struct list*, l_prev) *
    data_at(l->next, struct list*, t) *
    dlistrep_shape(t, l)

```

But this uses existential `t`, which can't appear in the invariant.

- `data_at(p_284_addr, ..., old_p_prev)` — p updated

I(new_p) = `data_at(&(old_p_prev->prev), X) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, old_p_prev) * data_at(&(old_p_prev->next), Y) * dlistrep_shape(Y, old_p_prev)`

To match, QCP needs to:
1. Find `data_at(&(old_p_prev->prev), X)` from S_post
2. Find `dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, old_p_prev)` by splitting the existing `dllseg_shape(head, 0, old_p_prev, old_p)`
3. Find `data_at(&(old_p_prev->next), Y)` from S_post
4. Find `dlistrep_shape(Y, old_p_prev)` by combining `data_at(&(old_p->next), old_p_next)` and `dlistrep_shape(old_p_next, old_p)`

For step 2: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, old_p_prev, old_p)` splits into:
`dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, old_p_prev) * data_at(old_p_prev->next, old_p) * data_at(old_p_prev->prev, X)`
where X is old_p_prev->prev.

This gives us `data_at(old_p_prev->prev, X)` and `data_at(old_p_prev->next, old_p)` and `dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, old_p_prev)`.

For step 1: `data_at(&(old_p_prev->prev), X)` matches `data_at(old_p_prev->prev, X)` from the split. ✓

For step 3: `data_at(&(old_p_prev->next), Y)` should match with `data_at(old_p_prev->next, old_p)`. So Y = old_p. ✓

For step 4: `dlistrep_shape(Y, old_p_prev)` = `dlistrep_shape(old_p, old_p_prev)`. Need to create this from S_post.

S_post has `data_at(&(old_p->next), old_p_next) * dlistrep_shape(old_p_next, old_p)`.

`dlistrep_shape(old_p, old_p_prev)` unfolds to:
`data_at(old_p->next, old_p_next) * data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev) * dlistrep_shape(old_p_next, old_p)`.

We have `data_at(old_p->next, old_p_next)` and `dlistrep_shape(old_p_next, old_p)` from S_post. We also have `data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev)` from the OLD invariant (which was consumed for the body). Wait, was it consumed?

The body reads `p->prev`. The `data_at(&(p->prev), old_p_prev)` is consumed by the read. After consumption, S_post does NOT have `data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev)`.

But we also have `data_at(old_p_prev->next, old_p)` from the segment split (step 2). This tells us `old_p_prev->next = old_p`, but NOT `old_p->prev = old_p_prev`.

Hmm, we need `data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev)` for folding `dlistrep_shape(old_p, old_p_prev)`. But it was consumed!

Actually, wait. `data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev)` = `data_at(&(old_p->prev), old_p_prev)`. This was created by the field expression `p->prev` in the OLD invariant (before the body). It was consumed by the read `p->prev` in the body.

So we DON'T have it in S_post. And the NEW `p->prev` would now be `data_at(&(old_p_prev->prev), X)`, not `data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev)`.

So `data_at(old_p->prev, old_p_prev)` is gone. Can we derive it from S_post without it?

`dlistrep_shape(old_p, old_p_prev)` requires `old_p->prev = old_p_prev`. But we don't have this as a separate data_at.

From the segment split: `data_at(old_p_prev->next, old_p)` tells us `old_p_prev->next = old_p`. In a doubly-linked list, this implies `old_p->prev = old_p_prev`. But is this implication explicit in QCP's reasoning?

The segment's definition doesn't directly state `old_p->prev = old_p_prev`. It states that the last node inside the segment (`old_p_prev`) has `next = endpoint (old_p)`. The implict property that `endpoint->prev = last_node_inside` is NOT part of the `dllseg_shape` definition.

So QCP cannot derive `old_p->prev = old_p_prev` from `dllseg_shape` alone. This means the folding `dlistrep_shape(old_p, old_p_prev)` fails.

So this approach also has a fundamental folding issue.

OK I think I need to go back to the fundamentals and try a COMPLETELY different structure.

What if the invariant doesn't require folding at all and just tracks a full list?

`dlistrep_shape(head, 0)` — full list, no sliding parts.

For Memory Read: the invariant must provide `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)`. Since `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)` has `head` at arg[0], not `p`, QCP can't directly read `p->prev`.

UNLESS QCP unfolds `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)` until it finds `p`, then reads `p->prev`.

From the log, the full-list approach failed with Memory Read error. But what if I also add `has_permission(&p)` to provide the missing permission?

Earlier, `has_permission(&p)` caused `dup_data_at_error`. But maybe the combination with `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)` doesn't cause `dup_data_at` because `p` is NOT an arg[0] of any predicate?

Wait, with `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`, the list is fully covered by the predicate. `has_permission(&p)` would cover the struct at address `p` (which is inside the list). When QCP unfolds `dlistrep_shape` to reach `p`, the `data_at` fields for `p`'s node would conflict with `has_permission(&p)`.

Actually, `has_permission` is NOT field-level — it's a coarse permission for the entire pointer. When QCP unfolds `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)`, it produces `data_at(p->next, ...)` and `data_at(p->prev, ...)`. These field-level `data_at` might not conflict with `has_permission(&p)` if `has_permission` is just a read permission, not an ownership claim.

The error from the has_permission attempt showed `dup_data_at_error (p_284_addr)`. `p_284_addr` is the ADDRESS of variable `p`, not the address that `p` points to. `p_284_addr` is the stack address of `p`. `has_permission(&p)` grants permission to the stack cell of `p`, not to the struct `p` points to.

Wait, `&p` in C is the address of the variable `p`, which is on the stack. `has_permission(&p)` in QCP would grant permission to the stack variable `p`. The `dup_data_at_error (p_284_addr)` means the stack cell `p_284_addr` has two owners.

But `dlistrep_shape(head, 0)` doesn't include `data_at(p_284_addr, ...)`. The stack variable's `data_at` is separate. Where does the duplicate come from?

Looking at the earlier attempt, the `dup_data_at_error` appeared alongside `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. The predicates own heap cells (list nodes), not stack cells. The `has_permission(&p)` should cover the stack cell, not the heap cell.

But `p_284_addr` is the stack address of `p`. The `data_at(p_284_addr, ...)` is created by QCP for the stack variable `p`. The invariant already has `data_at(p_284_addr, ...)` through QCP's stack management. Adding `has_permission(&p)` creates another owner for the same stack cell.

So `has_permission(&p)` conflicts with QCP's automatic stack variable management. This is the `dup_data_at_error (p_284_addr)`.

The SKILL.md checklist says to use `has_permission` for pointer-typed variables whose names do NOT appear as first argument of any spatial predicate. But maybe `has_permission` overlaps with QCP's implicit stack variable management.

Looking at the example invariant in SKILL.md:
```
/*@ Inv Assert u == t->next && t != 0 &&
    dlistrep_shape(y, 0) *
    dlistrep_shape(u, t) *
    dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)
 */
```

No `has_permission` in the example! The variables `u`, `t`, `y`, `x` all appear in shape predicate arguments. Their permissions are handled by the shape predicates (implicitly through QCP's stack management).

So the rule is: variables must appear in shape predicate arguments. If they don't, `has_permission` can help, but it also causes issues.

This means my ONLY option is to have `p` in shape predicate arguments. And the field expression approach works for memory reads but fails for entailment.

But wait — the field expression approach failed for entailment because of double-counting. The field expression creates `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)` that's also inside `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`.

What if I use `p` in PREDICATE arguments but WITHOUT field expressions?

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, l_prev)`

At entry (p = l): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` ✓

After one iteration (p = l_prev): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(l_prev, l_prev)`

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev)` — this is the `yp == y` circular case. `l_prev->next = l_prev`. But actually `l_prev->next = l`, so this is WRONG.

Unless the segment is EMPTY: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l_prev)` = `(head == l_prev && 0 == l_prev && emp) || ...`. If `head == l_prev`, the segment is `emp`. Otherwise, it's non-empty with `l_prev->next = l_prev` (circular).

So this doesn't work when `head != l_prev`.

What if I use `dlistrep_shape` anchored at `p` and the segment tracks the traversed part?

Actually, let me reconsider. The loop traverses backward. The traversed part grows. What if the invariant is:

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, p, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, p)`

At entry (p = l): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l)`. 
`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l, l)`: if `head != l`, non-empty segment. `l` is both last node inside and endpoint → `l->next = l` (circular!). WRONG.

After one iteration (p = l_prev): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. This matches the precondition! ✓

After two iterations (p = y_295): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, y_295, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, y_295)`. But the actual state has y_295 as l_prev->prev.

Wait, this gets closer but fails at entry because `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l, l)` is circular.

What if the entry is the only problem and QCP handles it differently? At entry, `p = l = head`? No, if `l == head`, the loop doesn't execute.

If `l == head`, the loop condition `p != head` is false, so the loop exits immediately. QCP doesn't need to check the invariant for the loop body in this case. It only checks the invariant ENTRY, then checks the exit condition.

For the entry: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, head, head)` = segment from head to head with last node head. This is `(head == head && 0 == head && emp)` — the first condition of the base case is `head == head` (true) but `0 == head` is false (assuming head != 0). So the base case doesn't hold.

Non-empty case: `exists z, data_at(head->next, z) * data_at(head->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z, head, head, head)`. And `dllseg_shape(z, head, head, head)` recursively tries to reach base case: `z == head && head == head`, so we need `z == head`. But `z = head->next`, and `head->next != head` for a non-circular list. So this doesn't terminate naturally.

So `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p, l)` doesn't work at entry when p = l.

Hmm, what about `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p)` with `p` anchored at endpoint?

At entry (p = l): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` ✓
After one iteration (p = l_prev): `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev->prev, l_prev)` — l_prev->prev is a field expression.

The dlistrep_shape would be `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` or `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`.

OK I keep going back to the field expression approach. The field expressions are essential for tracking `p`. But they cause double-counting.

Let me think about this problem differently. What if the ANSWER is to accept the double-counting from field expressions and find an invariant that QCP CAN handle?

What if the invariant explicitly unfolds the first level of the predicates?

At entry p = l:
`data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * data_at(l->next, t) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

This is the unfolded form of `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. And the invariant IN THE UNFOLDED FORM would be:

`data_at(p->prev, X) * data_at(p->next, Y) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, p) * dlistrep_shape(Y, p)`

But I can't write `data_at` directly in the invariant (QCP doesn't allow user-written `data_at` in invariants). 

Actually wait, QCP DOES allow `data_at` in invariants. Let me check... the SKILL.md shows SEP items as shape predicates and `has_permission`. It doesn't explicitly say `data_at` is allowed. But in the goal file, the right side includes `data_at`. So QCP internally uses `data_at`, but can the user write it?

Actually, `data_at` is a QCP constructor. In the invariant syntax, the user writes spatial predicates. `data_at` is also a spatial predicate. Let me check if it can be in the user's invariant.

But wait, actually, I notice that in the goal file, the invariant is represented as:

```
EX (p_prev: Z) ,
[| (l_pre <> 0) |] 
&&  [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
&&  ((&((l_pre)  # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr  |-> p_prev)
**  (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre )
**  (dlistrep_shape l_pre p_prev )
```

The `((&((l_pre) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev)` is the `data_at` representation. This is QCP's internal form for `data_at(&(l->prev), struct list*, p_prev)`.

Can the USER write `data_at(&(p->prev), struct list*, ...)` in the invariant? I don't think so — `data_at` is an internal QCP construct, and the invariant uses the high-level assertion syntax.

OK, I'm stuck on the field expression issue. Let me try a completely different approach.

What if the invariant describes the list as a WHOLE and tracks `p` through `has_permission` but avoids the `dup_data_at` error by NOT including `p`-related predicates?

Wait, earlier I found that `has_permission(&p)` causes `dup_data_at_error (p_284_addr)`. This is because QCP automatically creates `data_at(p_284_addr, ...)` for the stack variable `p`, and `has_permission(&p)` adds another owner for the same stack cell.

What if I DON'T mention `p` in the invariant at all? Then `p` is dropped from the program state, and QCP can't read `p->prev`. So Memory Read error.

OK, let me try yet another creative approach. What if the invariant is symmetric: `dllseg_shape` from head to a midpoint, and `dlistrep_shape` from the midpoint forward?

The midpoint should be `l` (which is fixed). `p` is somewhere between head and l.

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

At entry: matches. After body: the heap doesn't change (only `p` changes). The invariant doesn't mention `p`, so it still holds. Memory Read: QCP needs to read `p->prev`. Since `p` is in PROP via `p != 0`, QCP needs to find `p` in the predicates.

But QCP can't match `p` to any argument. MEMORY READ FAILS.

`dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`

At entry: 
- Left: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
- Left unfolds dlistrep_shape: `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`

Right (invariant evaluated):

Right = `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

Left = `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

These MATCH 1-to-1! ✓

For the preservation entailment:
Before body: S = `data_at(p->prev, p_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * data_at(p->next, p_next) * dlistrep_shape(p_next, p)`

The body `p = p->prev` CONSUMES `data_at(p->prev, p_prev)` to read the value `p_prev` and assigns it to `p`.

After body, S' = `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * data_at(p->next, p_next) * dlistrep_shape(p_next, p)`
(Note: `p` is now `p_prev`, so `data_at(p->next, p_next)` with the new `p` is `data_at(p_prev->next, p_next)`)

Wait, no. The `data_at(p->next, p_next)` has a FIXED ADDRESS set by the OLD `p`. Changing `p` doesn't change the address — the address was already computed.

Actually, I think the issue is subtler. In QCP, `data_at(&(p->next), p_next)` is computed using `p`'s value. After `p = p->prev`, the address `&(p->next)` is re-evaluated with the new `p` value.

But the HEAP hasn't changed — only the stack variable `p` changed. So `data_at(new_p->next, ...)` refers to a DIFFERENT address than `data_at(old_p->next, ...)`. The old `data_at(old_p->next, ...)` is no longer valid because the address changed.

Hmm, I think the address is computed AT RUNTIME by QCP, not stored in the state. The state stores the predicate as `data_at(&(p->next), struct list*, p_next)`. When `p` changes, the address `&(p->next)` changes too.

But in QCP's representation, the address is a VALUE. `&(p->next)` = `p_value + offsetof(list, prev)`. After the body, `p_value` changes to `old_p_prev`. So `&(p->next)` changes. This means the old `data_at` no longer refers to the same location.

Actually, I think QCP treats the STATE as having the ACTUAL VALUES at each point. The `data_at(&(p->next), ...)` is NOT in the state as a symbolic expression — it's evaluated to a concrete value. After `p` changes, the `data_at` for `p->next` would be RE-EVALUATED to a new concrete address.

Wait, but the state keeps `data_at(p_284_addr, struct list*, p_value)`. And `data_at(&(p->next), p_next)` would be something like `data_at(p_value + 0, struct list*, p_next)` in the state (assuming `next` is at offset 0 and `prev` is at offset 8 or whatever).

Hmm, I think the representation in QCP is literal: `data_at ( &(l@pre->next) , struct list* , y_290 )`. The `&(l@pre->next)` is the address of the `next` field of the struct at `l@pre`. This is a COMPUTED value.

When the invariant is EVALUATED, QCP computes `&(p->next)` using `p`'s current value. This creates a `data_at` with that address. The CONSUMING of this `data_at` happens during the body execution (when reading `p->prev`) or during the entailment check.

Actually, let me reconsider. The entailed goal is:

The LEFT (what we have before the body) is:
`[| p != head |] && [| p != 0 |] && [| l != 0 |] && data_at(p->prev, p_prev_2) ** dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p) ** data_at(p->next, p_next_2) ** dlistrep_shape(p_next_2, p)`

The RIGHT (what the invariant says after the body) is:
`EX p_next p_prev, [| p_prev_2 != 0 |] && [| l != 0 |] && data_at(p_prev_2->prev, p_prev) ** dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2) ** data_at(p_prev_2->next, p_next) ** dlistrep_shape(p_next, p_prev_2)`

Now, the LEFT has `p` (the old p), `p_prev_2 = p->prev`, `p_next_2 = p->next`.

The RIGHT has `p_prev_2` as the new "p" (since after the body, p = p->prev = p_prev_2).

To match:
1. `data_at(p->prev, p_prev_2)` on the left → consumed by the body read. Not in the state anymore.
2. `data_at(p->next, p_next_2)` on the left → `data_at(old_p->next, p_next_2)` with absolute address.
3. `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p)` on the left → segment from head to old_p.
4. `dlistrep_shape(p_next_2, p)` on the left → list from old_p->next.

After the body (p ← p_prev_2):
- The state has predicates 2, 3, 4 (1 was consumed).
- But `data_at(p->next, p_next_2)` is with OLD p. After p changes, the ADDRESS `&(p->next)` changes too. But the predicate is in the state with an absolute address, not a variable-dependent one.

Hmm, I think in QCP, when the invariant is evaluated, the field expressions are resolved to addresses and values. The resulting `data_at` has an ABSOLUTE address and an ABSOLUTE value. These persist in the state. Changing `p` doesn't change the absolute addresses in the state.

So after the body (p ← p_prev_2), the state has:
- `data_at(&(old_p->next), p_next_2)` — absolute address of old_p->next
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, old_p)` — segment from head to old_p
- `dlistrep_shape(p_next_2, old_p)` — list from old_p->next

(Note: `p_prev_2` is `p->prev`, `old_p` is the old `p`. But since `p` was updated, the state stores these as concrete values, not as `p`. Let me be more precise.)

In QCP's state representation:
- `data_at(p_284_addr, ..., p_prev_2)` — p's value updated to p_prev_2
- `data_at(addr_of_old_p_next, ..., p_next_2)` — old_p's next field
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, old_p_value)` — segment from head to old_p_value
- `dlistrep_shape(p_next_2, old_p_value)` — list from old_p->next

And `old_p_value` is the value `p` had before the body (which is `l` or whatever `p` was pointing to at that iteration).

Now, the RIGHT side (invariant with new p = p_prev_2):
- `data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), p_prev)` — prev field of p_prev_2
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2)` — segment from head to p_prev_2
- `data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), p_next)` — next field of p_prev_2
- `dlistrep_shape(p_next, p_prev_2)` — list from p_prev_2->next

For the entailment to work:

Step 1: Split `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, old_p_value)` into:
`dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, p_prev_2) * data_at(p_prev_2->next, old_p_value) * data_at(p_prev_2->prev, X)`

Where X = p_prev_2->prev.

This gives us:
- `data_at(p_prev_2->prev, X)` → matches right's `data_at(p_prev_2->prev, p_prev)` with p_prev = X
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, X, p_prev_2)` → matches right's `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2)` ✓
- `data_at(p_prev_2->next, old_p_value)` → matches right's `data_at(p_prev_2->next, p_next)` with p_next = old_p_value

Step 2: Combine `data_at(old_p->next, p_next_2)` and `dlistrep_shape(p_next_2, old_p_value)` into `dlistrep_shape(old_p_value, p_prev_2)`.

But `dlistrep_shape(old_p_value, p_prev_2)` requires `old_p_value->prev = p_prev_2` and `old_p_value->next = p_next_2`.

From step 1, `data_at(p_prev_2->next, old_p_value)` gives `p_prev_2->next = old_p_value`. In a DLL, this gives us `old_p_value->prev = p_prev_2`. But this relationship isn't explicit in the predicates.

The data_at for `old_p_value->prev` is `data_at(old_p->prev, p_prev_2)` from the original state. But this was CONSUMED by the body (to read `p->prev`).

So we don't have `data_at(old_p_value->prev, p_prev_2)` in the state after the body. And QCP can't derive it from `data_at(p_prev_2->next, old_p_value)` alone (the DLL invariant is not automatically tracked).

This means `dlistrep_shape(old_p_value, p_prev_2)` can't be folded from the available resources.

So the preservation fails because `data_at(p->prev, ...)` is consumed by the body read, and the predicate folding needs it.

The fix: DON'T CONSUME `data_at(p->prev, ...)` during the body read. Or, make it available from another source.

In QCP, when reading `p->prev`, the `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)` is consumed. After the read, `p` is updated to the read value. The `data_at` is gone.

But what if the SAME information is available from BOTH the field expression AND the predicate? Then after consuming one (the field expression's data_at), the other (from the predicate) remains.

Wait, these are the SAME resource. The field expression `p->prev` creates `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)`. The predicate `dlistrep_shape(p, ...)` also contains `data_at(p->prev, ...)`. If they're the SAME `data_at`, then consuming it only happens once.

But in QCP, the field expression evaluation creates a NEW `data_at`. It doesn't "borrow" from the predicate. The predicate and the `data_at` are separate resources.

Hmm, but in the invariant check (entailment), QCP needs to match the RIGHT's `data_at` against the LEFT's resources. The LEFT has the predicate. When matching, QCP might "unfold" the predicate to produce the `data_at` and match it. But unfolding consumes the predicate.

So the conflict is:
- LEFT has `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` (among other things)
- RIGHT has `data_at(p->prev, p_prev) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` (double-counting the data_at)
- LEFT can produce `data_at(p->prev, p_prev)` by unfolding `dlistrep_shape`, but then remaining is `data_at(p->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, p)` which doesn't match the right's `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`

This is the fundamental conflict. The `data_at` from the field expression and the predicate are the SAME resource counted twice.

The solution from SKILL.md: replace the field expression with an existential variable (so no `data_at` is created). But the invariant can't introduce existential variables directly.

UNLESS — the invariant uses a logical variable from the function's `With` clause, and the existential introduced by QCP matches it.

Actually, looking at the SKILL.md example again:
```
/*@ Inv Assert u == t->next && t != 0 &&
    dlistrep_shape(y, 0) *
    dlistrep_shape(u, t) *
    dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)
 */
```

In this invariant, `t->prev` appears in `dllseg_shape` but NOT in `dlistrep_shape(u, t)`. The `dlistrep_shape` second argument is `t`, a variable. And `u == t->next` is in PROP, which creates `data_at(&(t->next), u)`.

Wait, `u == t->next` in PROP would also create a `data_at` for `t->next`! That's a field expression in PROP!

From SKILL.md section 3.4, Cause 2:
> Redundant equality PROP: u == t->next in PROP is a C expression that references t->next, causing QCP to create data_at(&(t->next), ...). If SEP already contains dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u), the predicate's parameters already encode it.

So `u == t->next` in PROP can ALSO create a `data_at` conflict! But the example uses it and it works...

Hmm, maybe the example has a specific structure where this doesn't cause issues.

OK let me try to find a pattern that works. The SKILL.md example uses:
1. `t->prev` in `dllseg_shape` arg[2] 
2. `u == t->next` in PROP (which creates `data_at` for `t->next`)

And it works. Why?

For our case, we have:
1. `p->prev` in `dllseg_shape` arg[2] 
2. `p->next` in `dlistrep_shape` arg[0]

In the example, `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` doesn't use `t->next` — it uses `u` (a variable) and `t` (a variable). The field expression `t->next` is in PROP, separate from any predicate that would contain the same `data_at`.

In our case, `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` uses `p->next` in arg[0]. This IS a field expression in a predicate argument. And `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` unfolds to `data_at(p->next->next, ...) * data_at(p->next->prev, p) * ...`. The `data_at(p->next->prev, p)` has a different address than `data_at(p->next, ...)`.

Wait, `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` starts at `p->next = t`. Unfolding:
`exists u, data_at(t->next, u) * data_at(t->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(u, t)`

The `data_at(t->prev, p)` is `data_at(p->next->prev, p)`. This is DIFFERENT from `data_at(p->next, ...)` because the addresses are different:
- `data_at(p->next, ...)` = address of the `next` field in node `p`
- `data_at(p->next->prev, ...)` = address of the `prev` field in node `p->next`

So the `data_at` created by `p->next` (the field expression) is at `&(p->next)`, which is the `next` field of the struct at `p`. And the `data_at` from unfolding `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` is at `&((p->next)->prev)`, which is the `prev` field of the struct at `p->next`. These are DIFFERENT addresses!

So using `p->next` in `dlistrep_shape` arg[0] does NOT create the same `data_at` as the field expression `p->next`. The field expression creates `data_at(&(p->next), ...)`, and the predicate's unfold creates `data_at(&((p->next)->prev), ...)`.

Wait, but in QCP, when the field expression `p->next` is EVALUATED in the context of the predicate argument, it creates `data_at(&(p->next), p_next)`. This is separate from what the predicate's unfold produces. The predicate uses `p->next` as the FIRST ARGUMENT, which is the ADDRESS of a node, not a field. So `p->next` is the NODE ADDRESS of the next node, not the ADDRESS OF THE `next` FIELD.

OK so `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` uses `p->next` as the STARTING NODE ADDRESS. QCP evaluates `p->next` to get the address `t`. Then the predicate is `dlistrep_shape(t, p)` which means a dlist starting at node `t`.

But QCP ALSO creates `data_at(&(p->next), t)` from the field expression `p->next`. This is a `data_at` for the `next` field of node `p`, containing the value `t`.

Now, this `data_at(&(p->next), t)` is PART of the invariant. In the entailment, the LEFT state needs to match it. The LEFT state has `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. When unfolded:
`data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

The `data_at(l->next, t)` matches `data_at(&(p->next), t)` when `p = l`. 

And the remaining `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)` needs to match the rest of the invariant: `data_at(&(p->prev), l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`.

Wait, the invariant is `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`. The field expressions create:
- `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev)` from `p->prev` in `dllseg_shape`
- `data_at(&(p->next), p_next)` from `p->next` in `dlistrep_shape`

And the predicates become:
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p)` where p_prev = p->prev's value
- `dlistrep_shape(p_next, p)` where p_next = p->next's value

With p = l:
Right = `data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * data_at(&(l->next), t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

Left (unfolded) = `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

These ARE the same! Match:
1. `data_at(l->next, t)` ←→ `data_at(&(l->next), t)` ✓
2. `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` ←→ `data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev)` ✓
3. `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` ←→ `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` ✓
4. `dlistrep_shape(t, l)` ←→ `dlistrep_shape(t, l)` ✓

ALL MATCH! So entail_wit_1 should be provable.

```
  EX (p_next: Z)  (p_prev: Z) ,
```

There are TWO existentials: `p_next` and `p_prev`. And the right side has two `data_at` items and two predicates.

`data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

The ORDER is different but the ITEMS are the same. In separation logic, order doesn't matter (it's a commutative monoid). So the entailment should hold.

The `p_284_addr` is the address of the stack variable `p`. `has_permission(&p)` creates a `data_at` for this stack address. QCP also creates `data_at(p_284_addr, ...)` for the variable `p`. These CONFLICT.

Actually, `has_permission(&var)` is meant for heap permission, not stack variable management. Looking at the SKILL.md:
> has_permission(&var) grants a pointer the right to be dereferenced/assigned.

This is about DEREFERENCING a pointer (accessing *p, p->field, etc.). It's not about the stack variable `p` itself.

So `has_permission(&p)` creates a permission to read the heap at address `p`. This is a HEAP permission, not a stack permission.

But the error says `dup_data_at_error (p_284_addr)`. `p_284_addr` is the stack address of `p`. So `has_permission(&p)` with `&p` = `p_284_addr` could be interpreted as granting permission to the STACK CELL of `p`.

In C, `&p` is the address of the variable `p`. In QCP, `has_permission(&p)` might mean permission for the stack variable at `&p`. This would conflict with QCP's automatic `data_at(p_284_addr, ...)` for the variable `p`.

So the issue is `has_permission(&p)` conflicts with QCP's variable management. The fix: DON'T use `has_permission(&p)`.

Actually, in QCP's entailment, the strategies try to match items from LEFT to RIGHT. If `dlistrep_shape` is matched first, it's consumed. Then `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` on the right has nothing to match with.

But if the strategies match `data_at` first: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` on the right tries to match against the left. The left doesn't have it, so the strategies unfold `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` to get it.

- `p->prev` = l->prev → creates `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` 
- predicates: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

So the right side is:
`data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

And the left is:
`dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`

The matching order MATTERS. If strategies match `dlistrep_shape` first:
- Left: `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` → Right: `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` → MATCHED, consumed
- Left: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` → Right: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` → MATCHED, consumed
- Left: (nothing left) → Right: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` → FAILED

If strategies match `data_at` first:
- Right: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` needs to match left
- Strateg doesn't find it on left
- Strategies unfold `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` → `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
- Now `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` matches ✓
- Remaining right: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
- Remaining left: `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l) * dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)`
- Match `dllseg_shape` on both sides ✓
- Remaining left: `data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
- Remaining right: `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
- This FAILS because data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l) ≠ dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)

So both orderings fail! The problem is that unfolding `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` produces `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`, but the right needs `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` which has been consumed.

The core issue is that `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` from the field expression IS A PART of `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`. Extracting it consumes the predicate, leaving a different form.

Why? Let me think about the strategy application order. Maybe the strategies don't try to unfold first. Instead, they try to match things in order.

Typical strategy for entailment:
1. Match left-to-right: try to match each left item against right items
2. If no match, unfold a left predicate and retry

If the strategies match `dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)` first (because it appears on both sides):
Left: `dllseg_shape` → consumed
Right: `dllseg_shape` → consumed

Then remaining:
Left: `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`
Right: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

Now, left has 1 item, right has 3 items. Left is `dlistrep_shape`. Strategies try to match it against right items. `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)` doesn't match `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` directly. So strategies unfold `dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)`:

Left after unfold: `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

Now left has 3 items, right has 3 items. But the ORDER might matter:
- Left: `data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
- Right: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * data_at(l->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`

The items are the same! Left has `data_at(l->next, t)` first, right has `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` first.

If the strategies match by position:
- Left[0] = `data_at(l->next, t)` vs Right[0] = `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)` → NO MATCH
- Fail!

If the strategies match by search:
- Try to match `data_at(l->next, t)` from left against ALL right items
- right has `data_at(l->prev, l_prev)`, `data_at(l->next, t)`, `dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
- `data_at(l->next, t)` matches `data_at(l->next, t)` ✓

Then remaining left: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
Remaining right: `data_at(l->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)`
ALL MATCH!

So if the strategies search (not positional match), the entailment works!

struct list *iter_back(struct list *l, struct list *head)
/*@ With l_prev
	  Require dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)
    Ensure  dlistrep_shape(__return, 0)
 */
{
    struct list *p;
    if (l == 0) {
      return head;
    }
  	else {
    	p = l;
     /*@ Inv Assert p != 0 && l != 0 &&
            dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) *
            dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)
      */
    	while (p != head) {
      	  p = p -> prev; 
      }
    }
    return p;
}

Looking at the current invariant:
```
/*@ Inv Assert p != 0 && l != 0 &&
        dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) *
        dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)
  */
```

The QCP tool processes `p->prev` in the dlistrep_shape argument as a field expression. This creates an implicit data_at item. Then:
1. The data_at for p->prev is created as a separate SEP item
2. dlistrep_shape(p, ?) is the remaining predicate
3. But to match the precondition/pre-state, the solver needs to unfold dlistrep_shape(p, ?) which gives another data_at for p->prev
4. Duplicate data_at → conflict

Wait, actually, looking at the entail_wit_1 goal:
```
forall (head_pre: Z) (l_pre: Z) (l_prev: Z),
  [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 l_prev l_pre)
  ** (dlistrep_shape l_pre l_prev)
|--
  EX (p_next: Z) (p_prev: Z),
  [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && ((&((l_pre)  # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev)
  ** (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre)
  ** ((&((l_pre)  # "list" ->ₛ "next")) # Ptr |-> p_next)
  ** (dlistrep_shape p_next l_pre)
```

Wait, this is the Coq VC generated for entail_wit_1. But WHERE does the invariant text enter? The invariant text creates specific VC witnesses. The QCP tool processes the invariant and generates:
- entail_wit_1: Does the initial state satisfy the invariant?
- entail_wit_2: Does the loop body preserve the invariant?
- return_wit_1: After loop exit, does the invariant imply the postcondition?
- etc.

So `entail_wit_1` is checking: does the precondition (dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev)) imply the invariant (dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)) with the appropriate substitution (p = l)?

But looking at the VC goal above, the invariant is unfolded into its component predicates. The `p->prev` field expression is turned into a data_at item and a fresh variable. This means the invariant:
```
dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)
```
is processed as:
```
∃ p_prev p_next. 
  [| l_pre ≠ 0 |] ∧ [| l_pre ≠ 0 |] ∧
  data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev) *
  dllseg_shape(head_pre, 0, p_prev, l_pre) *
  data_at(&(l_pre->next), p_next) *
  dlistrep_shape(p_next, l_pre)
```

With p = l_pre (substituted). So the field expression p->prev becomes:
- data_at(&(p->prev), ?) as a separate item
- The predicate uses a fresh variable for the field value

Now the entailment is: can we prove this from `dllseg_shape(head_pre, 0, l_prev, l_pre) * dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)`?

To match `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev)` and `data_at(&(l_pre->next), p_next)`, we need to unfold `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)` which gives:
```
data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev) *
data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next) *
dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)
```

Matching:
- `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev)` ↔ `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev)` ⇒ p_prev = l_prev ✓
- `data_at(&(l_pre->next), p_next)` ↔ `data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next)` ⇒ p_next = ?next ✓
- `dlistrep_shape(p_next, l_pre)` ↔ `dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)` ✓
- `dllseg_shape(head_pre, 0, p_prev, l_pre)` ↔ `dllseg_shape(head_pre, 0, l_prev, l_pre)` ✓

But the dlistrep_shape was CONSUMED by unfolding! After unfolding, we no longer have the original dlistrep_shape predicate. The unfolded parts give us what we need.

So the entailment SHOULD work. The issue must be that the strategy doesn't know to unfold dlistrep_shape in this context.

The invariant is: `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`

This means:
- Start from head with predecessor 0
- Go to p whose predecessor is p_prev
- From p (with predecessor p_prev) onward, the rest of the list

This captures the entire list from head to end, split at p.

For the invariance to hold across the loop body (p = p->prev):
- Before: p = current_position, with dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)
- After p = p->prev (moving backward): new_p = p_prev (assuming we read p->prev = p_prev from the list)
  - Wait, p_prev is the predecessor of p, so p_prev = p->prev
  - After p = p->prev, the new p is p_prev
  - We need: `exists new_p_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, new_p_prev, p_prev) * dlistrep_shape(p_prev, new_p_prev)`

- Unfold dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev) to get: 
  - data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev)  (the prev field of p)
  - data_at(&(p->next), ?next)   (the next field of p)

```
data_at(&(l_prev->prev), y_295) *
dllseg_shape(head, 0, y_295, l_prev) *
data_at(&(l_prev->next), l) *
data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev) *
dlistrep_shape(y_290, l) *
data_at(&(l->next), y_290)
```

These states are at the condition check (before entering or re-entering the body). At each step, MORE structure is exposed.

Wait, I think I misread the RPs. RP_iter_2 is at the SECOND iteration's condition check. But in terms of the loop structure:

```
p = l;
// RP_pre_loop: first time we reach the condition
while (p != head) {
    p = p->prev;
    // Then back to condition check:
    // RP_iter_2: second time at condition
    // RP_iter_3: third time at condition
}
```

So at RP_pre_loop, p = l.
At RP_iter_2, p has been updated once: p = l->prev = l_prev.
At RP_iter_3, p has been updated twice: p = l_prev->prev = y_295.

The invariant needs to hold at ALL three points.

But at RP_pre_loop, the state is fully folded (dllseg_shape * dlistrep_shape).
At RP_iter_2, dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev) has been unfolded once, exposing l's prev and next.
At RP_iter_3, further unfolded.

The invariant is about the state BEFORE the condition check. After each loop iteration, p = p->prev, which means:
- One node is "moved" from dlistrep_shape to dllseg_shape
- p moves backward by one node

The invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` says:
- At p = l: dllseg_shape(head, 0, l->prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l->prev)
  - l->prev = l_prev (from dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev))
  - So: dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev) ✓ (matches RP_pre_loop)

- At p = l_prev: dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(l_prev, l_prev->prev)
  - This needs l_prev->prev which is inside the dllseg_shape

The problem is that at RP_iter_2, the state has:
```
dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) * data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev) * data_at(&(l->next), y_290) * dlistrep_shape(y_290, l)
```

But the invariant expects:
```
dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev->prev, l_prev) * dlistrep_shape(l_prev, l_prev->prev)
```

These are NOT the same! At RP_iter_2:
- The dllseg_shape ends at l (not l_prev)
- The dlistrep_shape starts from y_290 (not l_prev)
- The current p = l_prev is between the dllseg_shape and the dlistrep_shape
- l is the "buffer" node: it was in the dlistrep_shape, got its fields extracted, and now sits between the dllseg_shape and dlistrep_shape

So the invariant doesn't match RP_iter_2 because at the iteration point, l has been partially unfolded but the invariant expects a clean split at p.

This is a fundamental issue with the invariant pattern. The loop moves p backward, but the unfolding happens differently.

Wait, actually, let me reconsider. RP_iter_2 is at the CONDITION CHECK of the SECOND iteration. At this point, p = l_prev. But the state shows that l has been unfolded. Why was l unfolded?

The answer is: because the loop body `p = p->prev` reads `p->prev` from the list. This is a memory READ. For QCP to verify this read, it needs `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)` in the current state. The strategies unfold `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` (where p = l, p_prev = l_prev) to expose the prev field. After the read, p becomes l_prev (the value read). But the unfolding leaves the rest of dlistrep_shape unfolded.

So after the loop body:
- dlistrep_shape(l, l_prev) is unfolded to: data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev) * data_at(&(l->next), ?next) * dlistrep_shape(?next, l)
- p is now l_prev

And the state before the NEXT condition check (RP_iter_2) is:
```
data_at(&(l->prev), l_prev) *
data_at(&(l->next), y_290) *
dlistrep_shape(y_290, l) *
dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)
```

Now, for the invariant to hold, we need to REMEMBER the unfolded state and reframe it. The invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` with p = l_prev needs:
- p->prev = l_prev->prev = something
- dlistrep_shape(l_prev, l_prev->prev)

From the current state:
- l_prev->prev = ? This is inside dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l)
- From dllseg_shape definition: this is the segment head → ... → l_prev → l
- l_prev's position in this segment: l_prev is the SECOND-TO-LAST element (followed by l)
- l_prev->next = l (so the segment covers head → ... → l_prev → l)

So l_prev->prev is inside the segment. For dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l), when we process from the right end:
- The segment goes from head to l
- l->prev = l_prev (given by the 3rd parameter)
- l_prev->prev is accessible by unfolding the segment further

Can we reframe dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) as dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev->prev, l_prev) * some linked data_at items?

dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) from the left:
- head->prev = 0
- head->next = n1
- dllseg_shape(n1, head, l_prev, l)

Eventually we reach: dllseg_shape(l_prev, something, l_prev, l) where the inner segment is:
- If l_prev ≠ l (which is true): data_at(&(l_prev->prev), something) * data_at(&(l_prev->next), l) * dllseg_shape(l, l_prev, l_prev, l)
- dllseg_shape(l, l_prev, l_prev, l): x = l, xp = l_prev, yp = l_prev, y = l → x = y → emp

So dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l) fully unfolds to:
- Sequence of nodes from head down to l_prev-1's next
- data_at(&(l_prev->prev), pred_of_l_prev) * data_at(&(l_prev->next), l) * emp
  
Where pred_of_l_prev is the node before l_prev in the chain.

So from dllseg_shape(head, 0, l_prev, l), we can extract:
- data_at(&(l_prev->prev), pred_of_l_prev)
- data_at(&(l_prev->next), l)
- The segment from head to pred_of_l_prev: dllseg_shape(head, 0, ?, pred_of_l_prev) — hmm, this requires continued unfolding

This is getting complex. The key issue is: can the QCP strategies do this reframing automatically?
