QCP_VERIFIED = NO (since 3 > 0 Admitted in proof_manual.v).

Now I need to diagnose the failures and fix them. Let me analyze each:

**entail_wit_1**: Entry check
```
forall (head_pre: Z) (l_pre: Z) (l_prev: Z),
  [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 l_prev l_pre)
  ** (dlistrep_shape l_pre l_prev)
|--
  EX (p_prev: Z),
  [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && ((&((l_pre) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev)
  ** (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre)
  ** (dlistrep_shape l_pre p_prev)
```

This needs to derive data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev) from dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev), with p_prev = l_prev. But the goal has TWO copies of `[| (l_pre <> 0) |]` — that's a redundant PROP that might cause issues.

The key issue: the invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` uses field expression `p->prev`. QCP translates this to:
```
EX (p_prev: Z),
  [| (l_pre <> 0) |] && [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && ((&((l_pre) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev)
  ** (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre)
  ** (dlistrep_shape l_pre p_prev)
```

The duplicate PROP `[| (l_pre <> 0) |]` is suspicious — QCP produces it twice. This could be because the `l != 0` appears in the Inv PROP and also as a predicate unfolding condition, causing a duplication.

But more importantly, the entailment needs:
1. From `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)`, unfold to get `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev)`
2. From the unfolded data_at, match with `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev)` with `p_prev = l_prev`
3. From the unfolded dlistrep_shape, get `dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)` — hmm wait, the goal has `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` which is `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)`. But unfolding dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev) gives `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev) * data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next) * dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)`. After consuming data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev) and data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next), what's left is `dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)`, NOT `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)`.

So the goal has `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` but unfolding gives `dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)`. These don't match!

The issue is: the field expression `p->prev` in `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` makes QCP:
1. Create `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev)` — takes the prev field out
2. Leave `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` — but this no longer has the prev field!

When we try to derive this from the precondition `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)` (folded), we unfold it which gives:
- data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev)
- data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next)
- dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)

The data_at matches, but dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre) ≠ dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev).

Unless the predicate can be refolded: dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre) * data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next) * data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev) can be folded back to dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev) = dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev). But the data_at items are already consumed by the goal's data_at items.

So the fundamental issue is the **double allocation problem**: the field expression takes one data_at out of the predicate, but the remaining predicate should be dlistrep_shape(p->next, p) not dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev). 

Actually wait — the invariant is `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`. When QCP processes this, it creates data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) (where p_prev is a fresh variable for the field value). The remaining predicate should be `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` or something equivalent. But the goal shows `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` which is WRONG — it keeps the original first argument but replaces the second.

I think the issue is that QCP's processing of field expressions in predicate arguments is:
- Replace `p->prev` with a fresh variable `p_prev`
- Add `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev)` as a SEP item
- Keep the predicate as `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` — note: the first arg `p` is unchanged, only the field expression arg is replaced

But this is semantically wrong! If we took `p->prev` out of the predicate, the predicate doesn't own it anymore. After unfolding `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`, we'd get:
- data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) — from the invariant text
- data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) — from unfolding dlistrep_shape
  
Which IS a dup_data_at! But the verify log shows no dup_data_at error...

Oh wait, maybe the QCP strategies handle the field expression differently than I thought. Maybe QCP:
1. Replaces `p->prev` with a fresh variable `p_prev` in the predicate
2. DOESN'T add data_at in the invariant text
3. Instead, the data_at is produced by unfolding during verification

But the goal.v shows explicit data_at... let me re-check.

Looking at entail_wit_1:
```
EX (p_prev: Z),
  [| (l_pre <> 0) |] && [| (l_pre <> 0) |]
  && ((&((l_pre) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev)
  ** (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre)
  ** (dlistrep_shape l_pre p_prev)
```

The `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev)` is an explicit part of the goal SEP. And `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` is the remaining predicate.

From the precondition:
```
(dllseg_shape head_pre 0 l_prev l_pre)
** (dlistrep_shape l_pre l_prev)
```

To match, we need to derive the goal's SEP from the precondition's SEP. The precondition has `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)` but the goal needs `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev) ** ... ** dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)`.

If we unfold `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, l_prev)`:
- data_at(&(l_pre->prev), l_prev)
- data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next)
- dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)

We can match data_at: l_prev = p_prev.
We can't directly match `dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)` with `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)`.

But we also have `data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next)` as an extra item.

For this to work, we'd need:
- dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre) * data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next) * data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev) = dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)

The data_at items would be consumed by folding back into dlistrep_shape, and dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre) would need to match the rest.

But the goal only has `data_at(&(l_pre->prev), p_prev)` and `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` — it does NOT have `data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next)`. So we'd need to somehow get rid of the data_at(&(l_pre->next), ?next).

AH — I think I see. The QCP strategy for handling field expressions in predicate arguments works like this:

The invariant `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` is translated to something like:
- `∃ p_new. data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` — where p->next is ALSO field expression!

Wait, no. Let me re-read the goal.v:

```
((&((l_pre)  # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr  |-> p_prev)
**  (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre )
**  (dlistrep_shape l_pre p_prev )
```

The dlistrep_shape is `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` where l_pre is the first arg and p_prev is the second. If we think about it:
- The field expression `p->prev` is in the SECOND argument of dlistrep_shape
- When the predicate unfolds, it gives: `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) * data_at(&(p->next), ?next) * dlistrep_shape(?next, p)`
- The data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) matches the explicit data_at from the invariant
- But the remaining dlistrep_shape starts at ?next, not at p

So the issue is clear: when we unfold `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` (with p_prev being the second arg), we get:
- data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) — matches the explicit data_at
- data_at(&(p->next), ?next) — extra item!
- dlistrep_shape(?next, p) — starts at a different node!

The goal has `dlistrep_shape(l_pre, p_prev)` (same first arg) but the unfold gives `dlistrep_shape(?next, l_pre)`.

This is the semantic mismatch. The unfolded predicate starts at a different node than the one in the goal.

This means the field expression approach is fundamentally incompatible with the strategy's unfold semantics for dlistrep_shape. The fix, as SKILL.md suggests, is to use `exists` instead.

But wait — the `exists` approach also has the problem: how does the entailment work?

With `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`:

entail_wit_1 becomes (with p = l_pre):
```
EX (p_prev: Z),
  (dllseg_shape head_pre 0 p_prev l_pre)
  ** (dlistrep_shape l_pre p_prev)
```

From the precondition:
```
(dllseg_shape head_pre 0 l_prev l_pre)
** (dlistrep_shape l_pre l_prev)
```

This is trivially satisfied by instantiating p_prev = l_prev! ✓

entail_wit_2 (preservation, p transitions to p->prev):
Before: `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`
After body (p = p->prev): need `exists q_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, q_prev, p_prev) * dlistrep_shape(p_prev, q_prev)` where p_prev = p->prev = the value read from p->prev

From the invariant before: unfolding dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev) gives:
- data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) — the read gets value p_prev
- data_at(&(p->next), ?next)
- dlistrep_shape(?next, p)

After p = p_prev (new value of p), we need:
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, q_prev, p_prev)` — this should come from `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p)` unfolded from the right... 

Hmm, this is still tricky. From the dllseg_shape head, 0, p_prev, p, when we unfold from the left, it eventually gives us the predecessor of p_prev. But the predicate structure doesn't directly support right-unfolding.

Wait, but looking at the entail_wit_2 goal from the current run:
```
forall (head: Z) (p_prev_2: Z) (l: Z) (p: Z),
  [| (p <> head) |]
  && [| (p <> 0) |]
  && [| (l <> 0) |]
  && ((&((p) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev_2)
  ** (dllseg_shape head 0 p_prev_2 p)
  ** (dlistrep_shape p p_prev_2)
|--
  EX (p_prev: Z),
  [| (p_prev_2 <> 0) |]
  && [| (l <> 0) |]
  && ((&((p_prev_2) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev)
  ** (dllseg_shape head 0 p_prev p_prev_2)
  ** (dlistrep_shape p_prev_2 p_prev)
```

This is the preservation check. The left side has:
- data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev_2) — the prev field of p
- dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p) — segment from head to p (with predecessor p_prev_2)
- dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev_2) — list from p

The right side needs:
- data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), p_prev) — the prev field of p_prev_2
- dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2) — segment from head to p_prev_2
- dlistrep_shape(p_prev_2, p_prev) — list from p_prev_2

This makes sense! After p = p->prev, p becomes p_prev_2 (the old prev). The new p (p_prev_2) needs its own prev field exposed for the next read.

To prove this entailment:
1. Unfold `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev_2)` at position p:
   - data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev_2) — already there ✓
   - data_at(&(p->next), ?next_p)
   - dlistrep_shape(?next_p, p)
   
   Wait, but we also need to handle dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p). When we unfold from the LEFT:
   dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p):
   - If head ≠ p_prev_2: unfold to data_at(&(head->prev), 0) * data_at(&(head->next), ?next_h) * dllseg_shape(?next_h, head, p_prev_2, p)
   - Continue until we reach p_prev_2
   - Then: ... * dllseg_shape(p_prev_2, ?, p_prev_2, p)
   - If p_prev_2 ≠ p: unfold to data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), ?) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), p) * dllseg_shape(p, p_prev_2, p_prev_2, p)
   - dllseg_shape(p, p_prev_2, p_prev_2, p): x = p, xp = p_prev_2, yp = p_prev_2, y = p → x = y → emp

So full unfolding of dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p) gives:
- Chain from head to p_prev_2-1
- data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), pred_of_p_prev_2)
- data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), p)

Then we can fold dllseg_shape(head, 0, pred_of_p_prev_2, p_prev_2) from the remaining chain (from head to pred_of_p_prev_2).

This should work! The strategy should handle it.

But the result shows it's Admitted — the strategies didn't prove it. Why?

Looking at the strategies applied in the verify log (from the earlier successful test), the strategies DO fire:
- dll_nodata rule 15, rule 6
- builtin rules

And the goal reduces to emp. So the strategies CAN handle it during symbolic execution.

But then why is it Admitted in proof_manual.v?

I think the issue is: the symexec strategies can discharge the entailments internally (as part of the symbolic execution), but they don't generate Coq proof scripts. The proof_manual.v is supposed to contain Coq proofs that can be checked independently. Since the strategies don't generate Coq tactics, the proofs are left as Admitted.

So the QCP symexec verification succeeds (by the strategies' internal decision procedure), but the Coq proof outputs are incomplete. The SKILL.md pipeline checks proof_manual.v for Admitted, which is a proxy check for whether the verification is complete.

If this is the case, then the issue is NOT fundamentally about the invariant formulation — it's about whether the strategies generate Coq proofs or not. The strategies succeed but don't produce Coq proof terms.

In that case, maybe I need to use a different invariant formulation that the strategies can both prove AND generate Coq proofs for.

Or maybe the issue is that the field expression causes the goal to be structured in a way the strategies' Coq proof generation can't handle.

Let me try the `exists` approach instead. The SKILL.md explicitly recommends this.

  - data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), old_p)

Wait, that doesn't work. dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, old_p) means:

- The segment goes from head to old_p

When fully unfolded from the left:
- Eventually we reach a point where the current node is p_prev_2

- Since p_prev_2 ≠ old_p: data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), ?) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), old_p) * dllseg_shape(old_p, p_prev_2, p_prev_2, old_p) = data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), ?) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), old_p)

So dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, old_p) = dllseg_shape(head, 0, q_prev, p_prev_2) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), q_prev) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), old_p)

= data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), q_prev) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), old_p) * data_at(&(old_p->prev), p_prev_2) * data_at(&(old_p->next), ?next) * dlistrep_shape(?next, old_p)

- dlistrep_shape part gives: data_at(&(old_p->prev), p_prev_2) * data_at(&(old_p->next), ?next) * dlistrep_shape(?next, old_p)

So the full state is: items from dllseg_shape unfold + items from dlistrep_shape unfold

dlistrep_shape(p_prev_2, q_prev) = data_at(&(p_prev_2->prev), q_prev) * data_at(&(p_prev_2->next), old_p) * data_at(&(old_p->prev), p_prev_2) * data_at(&(old_p->next), ?next) * dlistrep_shape(?next, old_p)

Actually, let me re-think the whole problem. The fundamental requirement is:
1. The invariant must allow reading `p->prev` in the loop body
2. The invariant must hold at loop entry, be preserved, and imply the postcondition

For point 1, the invariant must put `p` at arg[0] of some shape predicate so the strategies can unfold it and expose `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)`.

For point 2, the invariant should match all RP states.

```

Interesting! The left side has:
- `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev_2)` — from unfolding `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` in the invariant (reading p->prev for the loop body)
- `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` — after unfolding dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev), y is p->next
- `data_at(&(p->next), y)` — p's next field
- `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_2, p)` — the segment from head to p

Wait, that's different from what I expected. The left side has `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` NOT `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev_2)`. This is because the field expression `p->prev` in the OLD invariant created `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev_2)` and left `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev_2)`. But with the `exists` approach, the invariant is `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`.

When the loop body `p = p->prev` is processed:
1. QCP needs to read `p->prev` — this requires unfolding `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` where p_prev is the existential variable
2. Unfolding dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev):
   - data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev) — the prev field, reading gives value p_prev
   - data_at(&(p->next), ?next) — the next field
   - dlistrep_shape(?next, p) — the rest

So after the read, the state becomes:
- data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev_2) [where p_prev_2 = p_prev from the unfold, renamed to avoid confusion]
  
Wait, I'm confused by the variable names. Let me re-read:

The entail_wit_2 goal:
- Left side: state AFTER the loop body executes `p = p->prev`

    - `data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev_2)` — p is still the OLD p in this data_at, because it was generated BEFORE p was updated
    - `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` — where y = p->next (old p's next)
    - `data_at(&(p->next), y)` — p's next

- The invariant before the body: `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`
- The body reads `p->prev`, unfolding dlistrep_shape to expose data_at(&(p->prev), p_prev)

Let me look at the left side more carefully:
```
((&((p) # "list" ->ₛ "prev")) # Ptr |-> p_prev_2)
** (dlistrep_shape y p)
** ((&((p) # "list" ->ₛ "next")) # Ptr |-> y)

But there's no such data_at on the right side. The right side only has:
```
EX (p_prev: Z),
[| (p_prev_2 <> 0) |] 
&& [| (l <> 0) |]
&& (dllseg_shape head 0 p_prev p_prev_2)
** (dlistrep_shape p_prev_2 p_prev)
```

There's no data_at on the right side for rule 14 to match.

So the strategies can't use rule 14 to derive `p_prev_2 ≠ 0`.

I think the fundamental issue is that the strategies are designed for algebraic reasoning about separation logic predicates, not for deep semantic properties of list structures. The `p_prev_2 ≠ 0` property requires understanding that only the head node has a null predecessor in a well-formed doubly-linked list.
